We think the capex budget might gradually shift to AI compute
and AI networking in 2028, as memory usage needs to be
optimized. We see positives for TSMC and UMC.
TSMC 3Q26 earnings preview: We expect the company to raise full-year revenue
guidance to the low-to-mid-40% range (vs.  above 40%) Y/Y, implying 4Q26 revenue

will rise 10%-15% Q/Q amid very strong 3nm demand. 3Q26 gross margin should
reach the high end of guidance of 67.5% However, should Intel focus on internal
capacity expansion, we think TSMC would maintain 2nm capacity expansion at the
same pace and its capex at US$54bn for 2026. For 2027, we see TSMC’s capex
reaching US$75bn to US$80bn.
In terms of 2027 wafer price hikes, we see leading edge (7nm and below) up 5%-10%
and mature nodes up 0%-3%; hence, gross margin could approach 70% in 2027. As a

result, we raise  our TSMC price target  by 3% to NT$3,088.
Key topics for upcoming TSMC 3Q26 earnings call on October 15:
1. Would TSMC consider working with TereFab  (Taiwan News, Oct 3 2026) and
building new fab sites in Texas (Bloomberg, Oct 1 2026)? Our view is that expansion
in Texas could be necessary, considering customer demand and limited space at the
Arizona fab in five years.
2. If Intel were to increase capex, would that reduce outsourcing at TSMC? Our

recent industry checks suggest some trimming of Intel’s 3nm and 2nm; how might
TSMC reallocate its resources?
3. How would TSMC address large reticle size chip demand? Our checks suggest
MediaTek handled TPU v10 IceFish reticle size initially at 14x; could TSMC’s CoWoS
or CoPoS be able to support such packaging?
We raise  our UMC price target by 36% to NT$188 on AI networking (SiPh)
business upside: Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone

and PC demand weakness. We have seen some order cuts in PC semis recently, as
some consumer analog IC customers (e.g., Silergy) have not been able to pass
through UMC’s wafer price hike. Therefore, some investors do not expect any UMC’s
price hike in 2027. However, two growth drivers could help UMC raise wafer price
for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, as we noted in our earlier  Foundry
Floorplan, and 2) growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

TSMC's 2026–28 foundry floorplan broadly aligns with our
bottom-up demand analysis
Our industry checks suggest ASML allocates capacity based largely on historical demand
and long-term partnerships, indicating that TSMC remains the largest customer for EUV
shipments. If so, TSMC should retain strong pricing power and could increase leading-edge
wafer prices by 5%–10% in 2027, reflecting both the value it delivers to customers and the
potential for margin expansion.

TSMC 2nm and 3nm capacity growth remains robust; 5nm
set to decline
Consistent with our demand forecast, TSMC indicated very strong capacity requirements
for both 2nm and 3nm starting in 2026, implying a 70% capacity CAGR for N2 from 2026

to 2028. TSMC also suggested that 5nm capacity will begin to decline from 2027, in line
with our forecast that 5nm demand will drop as Blackwell transitions to Rubin on 3nm.
Separately, advanced packaging capacity expansion should remain in focus for TSMC in
2027. This includes, but is not limited to, CoWoS and SoIC, for which we now expect
capacity to reach 200kwpm and 40kwpm, respectively, by 2027.

TSMC to introduce initial 1.0nm capacity in Tainan in 2029. We have not baked in potential

How might TSMC address Intel's competition in both wafer
foundry and advanced packaging?
Intel EMIB-T remains competitive in 2029; how TSMC will
address?
With demand in AI continuing to boom, we expect TSMC and its OSAT partners to
expedite capacity expansion plans for CoWoS. We see TSMC's CoWoS capacity building
further to 260kwpm in 2028, from 130/200kwpm in 2026/2027, with 2028's expansion
mainly focused in Arizona's AP9/10 and potentially in AP7. TSMC is now conducting trial

production for CoPoS in Taoyuan. If the progress goes well, we would expect TSMC to
start building its CoPoS capacity, with location also likely to be in AP9/10 or AP7P3.
We expect the non-TSMC camp to expand CoWoS capacity to 110kwpm by 2028 year-end,
from 80kwpm by end-2027. For ASE/SPIL, we expect FoCoS+CoWoS capacity to rise from
30kwpm at 2027-end to 50-60kwpm at 2028-end, with expansion focused on CoWoS-L
and CoWoS-R. For Amkor, we expect CoWoS capacity to increase from 30kwpm at 2027-

end to 55-60kwpm at 2028-end, also focused on CoWoS-L and CoWoS-R.

What about  Intel EMIB capacity build?
For Intel, we see current average EMIB-M capacity of 110kwpm falling slightly to 95kwpm
in 2027 as it converts some toward EMIB-T. We see the major customers of EMIB-M being
Trainium3, upcoming Trainium 4 and its in-house server CPU. Average EMIB-T capacity is

around 5kwpm in 2026, which we expect to further scale to 15-20kwpm in 2027 and 40-
45kwpm in 2028. All of the capacity mentioned above is converted for  12-inch wafer
equivalent, which we believe would be much more easier to communicate with compared
to TSMC CoWoS capacity.
Although some small projects are ongoing, we still see MediaTek/Google Humufish
consuming most of the capacity during 2027-28. We maintain our view that Intel EMIB-T

could still  produce close to 3mn units of Humufish from late 2027-28, by assuming 4-5
chips per wafer of EMIB-T.

Indeed, we have seen some TSMC customers surveying (testing vehicles) Intel EMIB-T due
to the design for larger chip size, lower cost, and intention to seek a second source
because TSMC's CoWoS capacity is still relatively tight. However, we believe TSMC could
still try to expand its packaging size by leveraging different technologies to meet
customers' needs. We think it remains concerned about whether it is going to open its
front-end computing die production/know-how to its competitor, Intel, for back-end

packaging.
Our prediction of the trend after 2028 –  TSMC might:
1. Pull in CoPoS (panel-level process) with both 300mm and 500mm square panel
size
2. Introduce 3.5D packaging with SoIC for vertical chip stacking (Meta MTIA is
surveying)
3. Adopt glass core substrate to solve warpage for >9.7x reticle-size chips

Pros and cons of TSMC's CoWoS, Intel's EMIB, TSMC's
CoPoS, and SoIC
TSMC’s CoWoS, Intel’s EMIB, TSMC’s CoPoS, and TSMC’s SoIC represent different points
along the advanced packaging spectrum, each optimizing for a different balance of
bandwidth, cost, scalability and integration complexity. We think future design of the
chips is likely to combine these approaches—for example, using CoWoS for lateral

integration and SoIC for vertical stacking—to achieve optimal system-level performance.

In summary,

### Page 9

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 9
• CoWoS excels in high-bandwidth 2.5D integration but is costly and capacity-limited
• EMIB offers a more cost-effective and modular alternative with some performance
trade-offs
• CoPoS targets large-scale, cost-efficient integration but is still maturing
• SoIC delivers the highest performance through 3D stacking at the expense of
complexity and thermal constraints.

Idea
10
UMC is well positioned for Silicon Photonic IC (PIC) foundry
In our previous Foundry Floorplan, we presented a constructive view on UMC given its AI-
related revenue. Management indicated AI revenue of US$300mn this year (3-5% of
revenue), potentially more than tripling to US$1bn in three years (2029).
UMC has a  capacity expansion plan to capture AI demand, including a clean room for
advanced packaging for AI chips, such as wafer-on-wafer and memory-on-memory stacking.

Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone and PC demand
weakness, and we have seen some order cuts of PC semis recently. Some think a UMC
price hike in 2027 wouldn’t go through, but we see two growth drivers helping it
successfully  hike wafer price for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, and 2)
growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its 400Gb per lane solution
(PIC + TFLN) solution.
How UMC is differentiated in PIC foundry

According to UMC, InP and TFLN are the two most promising solutions, as they are much
more mature than the other contenders. InP is facing severe shortage, adding to TFLN’s
momentum.
UMC Group’s TFLN program started in 2021 with 6” in mass production and 8” in
sampling. TFLN's   strengths include low loss, high bandwidth (> 110 GHz), good thermal
stability, and strong linearity.
Integrating TFLN with PIC is a challenge as well as an opportunity. Advanced packaging

schemes and micro transfer printing are both seen as promising candidates. UMC is
developing the integration technology, leveraging its expertise in PIC, TFLN, and advanced
packaging.
Adoption of higher-performance modulators (TFLN/InP) and improvement in photo diodes
are key factors affecting deployment of plain-vanilla (non-WDM, IM/DD) DCIs with 400
Gbps/channel performance and beyond, which is very close to TSMC's mainstream PIC
technology Micro Ring.

Exhibit 15: UMC differentiates in TFLN process offering for PIC
Source: UMC

About Foundry Floorplan
In 2011,  we first published this product (Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan) to
provide our analysis of foundry industry dynamics. We have resumed this practice as
global foundry competition remains active. Foundry supply is also a critical resource for
future AI chips, whether in the global market or China's local market.
Below are prior reports from this series:
• The Foundry Floorplan: All boats riding on the same tide – mature node up-cycle

begins (19 May 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: TSMC preview update; CoWoS vs. EMIB competition (14
Apr 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Raise TSMC's 2027e and 2028e Capex (9 Mar 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Update on TSMC's 2027 capex; interposer outsourcing to
Vanguard? (13 Jan 2026)
• Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan, April 2011

TSMC: Estimate and price target revisions
Our earnings forecasts increase 4% for 2026,  5% for  2027 and 3% for 2028: We raise
our 2026 revenue forecast and modestly increase our margin assumptions to reflect
stronger-than-expected 3Q26 performance, driven by robust AI demand, including
sustained Blackwell shipments. We raise our 2027–28 revenue assumptions by 3%–5%,
reflecting the increase in strong leading-edge demand as well as the wafer price hike.

Our PT increases to NT$3,088 from   NT$2,988, driven primarily by our estimate

revisions. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027 EPS forecast. TSMC
currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of 16.5x since 2018. We
find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an

intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values increase to NT$3,720 (from NT$3,600) and NT
$1,700 (from NT$1,645), respectively.

UMC: Estimate and price target revisions
We raise our 2026, 2027, and 2028 earnings estimates by 56%, 25%, and 31%,
respectively: Our 2026e change is due mostly to strong 2Q26 non-op gains. We increase
2027/28e revenue and EPS due to new CPO related business contribution from 12-inch
SiPh PIC  as well as 8-inch TFLN. Our 2028e increase also includes DDI outsource from
Samsung foundry.

Risk Reward - UMC (2303.TW)
NT$188.00
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: 9.2% cost of equity, 13.5% medium-term

Morgan Stanley EstimatesMean
Consensus Price Target Distribution NT$38.30 NT$258.00
Source: Reﬁnitiv, Morgan Stanley Research
MS Rating
▪ We are OW as we see structural changes
in the mature node industry's
supply/demand dynamics.
▪ Its foundry quality is comparable to
TSMC's in mature nodes. Along with

specialty technologies, this could help offset
part of the margin downside.
▪ Non-AI market recovery remains uncertain
in 2027, yet UMC should still beneﬁt from
strong AI-related demand spillover from its
foundry peers.
▪ 12nm FinFET development is strategic. It
should be one of UMC's long-term
strengths, notably when Chinese foundries
cannot effectively expand FinFET capacity.

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

M
Update
Bizlink  | Asia Pacific
September Sales +5% MoM/
+39% YoY
In this report, we focus on Bizlink's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales came in at NT$9,332mn (+5% MoM/+39% YoY)
• 3Q26 revenue reached NT$27,854mn (+20% QoQ/+50% YoY), slightly short
of MSe of NT$28,521mn (+23% QoQ/+55% YoY) by 2% while exceeding
consensus of NT$27,423mn (+18% QoQ/+49% YoY) by 2%.
Our view:
• We think 3Q26 revenue came in largely in-line with market expectations.

• Looking into 2H26, we expect HPC to remain the main growth driver, as
power interconnects should continue to see momentum from AI/general
servers. Meanwhile, data interconnects should grow along with new ASIC
model ramp-up and GPU server demand. Moreover, semi cap equipment
revenue growth should also sustain.
• We believe Bizlink has allocated increasing resources toward the optical
interconnect space, including the collaboration on ALC with Credo, mini

VCSEL optical module with ams-OSRAM and shuffle box with Corning. We
think that these opportunities could help offset a potential slowdown in its
AEC business as AEC solutions reach their scalability limits at higher data
rates, likely from  3.2T.
• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Bizlink (3665.TW)
Base case, derived from 30x 2027e P/E. We think our target P/E multiple is justified by an
earnings CAGR of 46% seen over 2025-28e, which implies a PEG of around 0.7. This is in line
with the average of 0.7 in 2019-24, when the stock was trading at an average 13x P/E for a 19%
earnings CAGR.
Risks to Upside
n Faster and wider AEC adoption
n Data center and semi equipment capex increases that drive stronger HPC and semi-cap

business
n Stronger recovery for automotive business
Risks to Downside
n Lower-than-expected AEC adoption
n Intensifying competition
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower-than-expected growth in the semi capital equipment market
n Continued weakness in the auto industry

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

NYPCB

Alarm cleared with new drivers into 2027; 
re-it. Buy with PO raise to NT$2,000

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 2,000 TWD | Price: 1,340 TWD

BT substrate price hike resumed from 4Q26 
Following (see report) the worries of moderating BT substrate price hike in early 
September, we now expect NYPCB to still raise relevant ASP by single-digit % in 4Q26, 
and the hike is purely driven by favorable S/D which will fully translate into the accretion 
in bottom-line. Following this, BT substrate price will likely be raised by another 20+% in 
1Q27, due partly to the cost pass-through. We currently bake in ~10% revenue QoQ with 
32-33% GM for 4Q26, but we do not rule out the possibility for its revenue to surprise 
on the upside, should there be a faster ramp of its ABF substrate capacity in Kunshan. 
New driver for 2027: server CPU & mix optimization  
Looking into 2027 we expect the benefits of demand overflow on (see report) server 
CPU side to materialize, and we flag a chance for the firm to record 20+% revenue from 
the emerging business with AMD server CPU, where NYPCB is working on the 
qualification in its Shulin site currently and should capture most of the spillover demand 
if the progress goes smooth. Meanwhile, the firm will likely allocate more capacity from 
other sites (e.g. Kunshan) to fulfill the demand from Broadcom, which also helps enhance 
the mix. Currently we model ~50% GM for its ABF substrate business in 2027, but we 
see chance for further upward revision, driven by more favorable S/D dynamics and mix.  
Raise estimates/PO; prime beneficiary of price hike 
NYPCB’s share price (up ~50%) lags its major peers Unimicron/Kinsus (up ~70%) since 
late-July, but in our view it is still the best proxy to position for price hike in the 
substrate market. Judging from (see report) Kinsus’ August prints last week, we believe 
NYPCB remains on track to meet or beat the market’s expectations on N-T margins, and 
we reiterate Buy on the stock. Along with 13%/18% EPS raise in 2027/28E thanks to the 
more benign pricing/margin outlook driven by further mix improvement and S/D, we 
raise PO to NT$2,000 (from NT$1,700) same based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Founded in 1997, NYPCB was listed on the Taiwan Stock 
Exchange in 2006. The firm is a 67%-owned subsidiary of 
Nan Ya Plastics Corporation (1303 TT). NYPCB's main 
products are HDI, PCB and IC substrates (including ABF and 
BT substrates), widely used in telecommunication, 
consumers, PC, and Auto applications. The clients include 
AMD, Broadcom, Samsung, Apple, Continental, etc. The 
main competitors include Unimicron, Kinsus, Ibiden, 
Shinko, and SEMCO, etc.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on NYPCB, in view of its continuous 
improvement of utilization rate and gross margin across 
ABF and BT substrate business into 2027, thanks to the 
robust demand growth from fabless customers and 
demand overflow in AI ASICs.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$2,000 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. We view this multiple as fair 
given ROE and operating margin expansion to 39% and 41% in 2027-28E (vs 13% and 
14% in 2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment rating s are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial 
rating)

Underperform N/A ≥ 20%

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be 
secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend.  Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two  or more analysts sharing a common industry, 
sector, region or other classification(s). A stock’ s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기 환율 부진에도 영업이익 100조는 넘긴다 
삼성전자 26년 3분기 실적은 이전 전망치를 하회할 전망이다. 매출액은 195.2조원으
로 이전 분기 대비 13.8% 증가하고, 영업이익은 101.7조원으로 13.6% 증가할 것으
로 예상한다. 영업이익률은 52.1%로 전 분기 대비 0.1%p 하락할 전망이다. 원/달러 
환율 영향이 주요 변수로 작용한 것으로 추정한다. 2분기에 집행되지 않았던 성과급 
일부가 3분기에 반영된 영향도 있을 것으로 예상한다. DS 부문은 매출액 149.9조원, 
영업이익은 101.8조원으로 추정하고, 디스플레이 매출액 8.8조원, 영업이익 0.9조
원, MX/네트워크는 매출액 34.3조원, 영업적자 1.1조원으로 예상한다. VD/가전도 
영업적자 규모가 커질 것으로 예상한다.  
주주환원은 주가 상승을 견인할 것 
8월 21일 이후 주주환원에 대한 관심이 커졌다. 자사주 규모는 크지 않겠지만 배당
을 통한 주주환원 규모는 사상 최대 규모일 것으로 예상한다. 글로벌 대표 IT업체와 
비교해도 손색없는 수준이다. 메모리 업체의 주주환원 규모가 100조원 넘게 집행된 
적이 없어서 주가에 어떠한 영향을 미칠 지가 관전 포인트인데 긍정적 역할을 할 것
으로 기대한다. 자사주를 통한 EPS 개선은 아니지만, 안정적이고 지속적인 규모의 
주주환원은 주가를 한층 더 높은 수준에서 평가 받는 계기가 될 것으로 판단한다.  
현저한 저평가 구간, 이전 전망 유지 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 메모리 공급 부족 상황이 장기화될 
것으로 전망하고, 이로 인해 수익성은 현재 수준에서 유지되고, 이익 규모는 안정적
으로 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 현저히 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 목
표주가는 460,000원을 유지한다.

### Page 4

삼성전자 (005930)

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
반도체 Check Point 
1. 차별화된 주주환원 전략. 배경은 안정적 이익 규모 
2026년에는 대대적인 규모의 주주환원이 예상된다. 100 조원 내외 수준으로 
예상하는데 상당 부분은 배당으로 집행될 것으로 추정한다. 배당성향이 25%가 유지될 
것으로 예상하기 때문이다. 자사주 매입 여력은 20조원 내외 수준일 것으로 예상한다. 
주주환원은 주가 밸류 상승에 기여할 것으로 기대한다. 일회성이 아니고 장기적으로 
높은 수준의 주주환원이 가능하다는 것은 싸이클 변동에 따른 이익의 변동성이 
낮다는 의미이기도 하다. 
2. AI 모델 개발 속도 조절. 그래도 메모리 수요를 줄일 수는 없다 
Anthropic CEO는 현재 AI 개발 속도가 지나치게 빨라서 인간의 능력을 뛰어 넘는 
임계점에 이를 가능성이 높아져서 개발 속도를 늦출 필요가 있다고 언급했다. 이를 
Meta, Google, SpaceX 수장들이 공감했다. 우선 이들의 공감으로 AI 모델의 개발 
속도가 둔화될 것으로 보고 있지 않다. 또한, 이를 가정하더라도 향후 AI 모델은 
Agent AI로 진화할 것으로 보고 있다. 이에 따른 메모리에 대한 필요성은 급격히 
증가할 것으로 기대한다. 
3. 자신감 넘치는 HBM  
HBM4가 2026년 하반기부터 주력 제품이 될 것으로 전망한다. 26 년 3분기에는 
비중이 40% 내외, 4 분기에는 60%를 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 연초 기대 대비로는 
낮은 수준이다. 삼성전자의 점유율만 보면 연초 대비 높은 수준의 점유율이 예상된다.  
HBM4 물량이 예상보다 늦게 본격화되면서 2026년 HBM 시장 규모는 이전 전망 
대비 축소될 전망이다. HBM4도 늦은 출시로 연내 생산 물량은 이전 전망치인 130억 
Gb을 하회할 것으로 예상한다.  
2027년 HBM4 가격이 최근에 확정이 되었고, 이를 통해서 DRAM 수준의 수익성 
확보가 가능할 것으로 기대한다. 수익성 발목을 잡았던 HBM은 2027년 물량부터는 
영업이익 개선의 변수가 될 것으로 전망한다.

장기적 주주환원은 
안정적 이익이 뒷받침 
Agent AI는 메모리 
수요를 증가시킨다 
HBM4 안정적 진입한 
것으로 판단

Document 215: Consumer Checkpoint_ Still sizzling August spending remained robust as consumers sought value, income groups converged and household finances stayed healthy. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ Still sizzling August spending remained robust as consumers sought value, income groups converged and household finances stayed healthy. 2 min read.pdf

Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 9

### Page 1

13017580     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling 
14 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained solid in August. Total card spending per household rose 0.9% month-over-month and 4.5% year-
over-year, according to Bank of America internal data. Discretionary services continue to provide a steady foundation for this 
growth, despite moderation in a few categories. 
• Consumers still appear to be focused on value. Spending at general merchandise and big-box retailers continues to outperform 
more traditional retailers, as evidenced during the back-to-school season. This suggests households are actively seeking lower-
cost alternatives while maintaining spending. 
• Spending and wage growth have largely converged across income groups, though across generations, the largest "K" shape 
remains among Millennials. Household finances also remain healthy, with credit card utilization declining across most age and 
income cohorts. Lower- and middle-income households still hold elevated deposit balances across all age groups, suggesting 
spending should remain resilient.

A summer of spending  
Consumer spending looked very robust in August. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household jumped 0.9% month-over-month (MoM) on a seasonally-adjusted basis, following the 0.2% dip in July. And card 
spending rose 4.5% year-over-year (YoY), more than four times the average growth rate of 2025. Excluding gasoline, spending 
was up 3.7% YoY, almost 2.5 times the rate in 2025. Additionally, while August YoY growth rates have eased over the past two 
months, they remained above the first three months of 2026 (Exhibit 1).  
Exhibit 1: Total card spending per household rose 4.5% YoY in August and 3.7% YoY excluding gasoline, with seasonally-adjusted spending up 0.9%

Discretionary services spending has remained a steady contributor to overall momentum. As for necessities, rising gasoline 
spending lost steam, with its largest upward impact on spending occurring earlier in the year. However, recent increases in both 
oil and gasoline prices may present some renewed headwinds to consumers if they are sustained (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Discretionary services spending has remained a bedrock of growth

Discretionary spending: Price pressures bite in some areas 
Spending growth outpaced transaction growth across most discretionary categories 
Within discretionary categories there is some variation, though YoY spending growth has improved since the beginning of the 
year across most (Exhibit 3). Leisure and travel-related spending (lodging and airlines) have seen the largest improvements, likely 
in part reflecting the impact of the World Cup over the summer.

But rising global oil prices have also pushed up costs in areas like travel. Looking at transactions as a guide to spending volumes,

Consumers continue to pivot to big-box stores and wholesalers 
One “stand-out” among discretionary spending categories is general merchandise, where robust and rising transaction growth 
has largely mirrored its spending growth, up 4.7% and 5.6% YoY, respectively.   
In our view, this partly reflects consumers searching for value to offset the increase in gas prices, among other items (read more 
in The rise of value). This trend extends to back-to-school spending. In fact, while spending at traditional back-to-school retailers 
(like teen and children’s apparel) has declined this year, outlays have ramped up at general merchandise stores (Exhibit 6). This 
suggests to us that back-to-school spending was likely solid this year but just shifted to different channels.  
Exhibit 6: Solid spending growth for general merchandise, while more traditional back-to-school retailers, like teen and children’s apparel, have 
experienced a slight decline

Spending and wages keep converging, but it differs slightly by age 
Looking across income cohorts, total card spending growth has largely converged in recent months, effectively closing the “K” 
shape in overall spending (Exhibit 7). This is also true in discretionary spending: Bank of America card data shows lower-income 
households’ discretionary spending growth is recovering strongly. In August, discretionary spending rose 5.9% YoY for higher-
income households versus 5.7% YoY for lower-income counterparts, the smallest gap since January 2024 (Exhibit 8). Meanwhile, 
middle-income households’ discretionary spending also improved but lagged other income cohorts slightly, albeit still up a 
healthy 5.1% YoY.  
The closing gap in spending growth between higher- and lower-income households has been mirrored in strengthening after-tax 
wage growth (read more in The Institute Employment Report: August 2026). Lower-income households' wage growth outpaced 
that of other cohorts in August for the second consecutive month (Exhibit 9). 
Exhibit 7: Lower-income households’ total card spending growth is 
stronger than all income cohorts

While the “K-shaped” divergence in discretionary credit and debit card spending has closed, it likely persists in higher-cost items 
and services such as vehicles and moving (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?).  
We see two reasons for this. First, these types of purchases may require saving for down payments or larger borrowing (and may 
be more interest rate-sensitive), so it’s possible recent wage growth has yet to translate into bigger-ticket buys. Second, some 
of the improvement in after-tax wage growth may be due to temporary factors like lower tax withholdings, meaning some 
consumers may not feel comfortable signing up for higher-dollar and longer-term financial commitments.  
Gen Z, Millennials and Gen X have less room to borrow, but elevated deposits 
When it comes to overall financial health, we believe consumers remain on solid footing. One sign of this is that more consumers 
are paying off their entire credit card balance every month, both compared to last year and to pre-pandemic levels (read more in 
Consumer Checkpoint: The great convergence). And for those who do carry a balance (revolvers) from month to month, overall 
utilization rates have declined YoY and are near pre-pandemic levels (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: Credit card utilization for revolvers is on par with pre-pandemic levels and has declined YoY 
Median credit card utilization for credit card revolvers (monthly, index 2019 average = 100, below 100 means there has been a decrease relative to 2019 levels)

across all income cohorts. Even in August 2026, Gen Z, Millennial and Gen X utilization rates are still well above Baby Boomer’s 
2019 utilization rates (Exhibit 12). The good news is that most of these younger generations have seen borrowing capacity 
increase slightly since then. Millennials, however, at the margin have made the least progress, with utilization rates still at 2019 
levels.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

EMERGING ECONOMIES: WHY ARE FINANCING CONDITIONS HOLDING UP WELL AGAINST THE
ENERGY SHOCK?
François Faure
Change in 10Y domestic government bond yields (basis points)
SOURCE: INSTITUTE OF INTERNATIONAL FINANCE, LSEG, MACROBOND
Six months have passed since the start of America’s and Israel’s military intervention in Iran and Lebanon. What picture is emerging around
the impact of this external shock on the financing conditions of the major emerging economies and on exchange rate movements? And what

parallels can be drawn with the 2022 energy shock?
Emerging economies: greater financial resilience to the shock from the Middle East

Chart of the Week / 9 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Financing conditions and exchange rates are holding up better in 2026 than in 2022…
As far as the cost of domestic financing in local currency (by far the main source of government funding) goes, bond yields rose less sharply, particularly for
Asian countries, with Türkiye standing as the sole exception (Chart 1)1. The main explanation for this is that in 2022, faced with rapidly surging inflation, al-

most all central banks had raised their key interest rates. Conversely, since February, few have done so, and some have even continued their easing cycles.
However, spreads relative to benchmark yields (US or German) have narrowed for virtually all countries (apart from Türkiye), whereas in 2022, by
contrast, these spreads had widened for Central European countries.
This reflects the reaction of the markets, which are just as concerned, if not more so, about the deteriorating public finances in advanced economies as

they are about those in emerging economies. In fact, real bond yields 2 in the Asian countries in the sample are currently approximately the same value
as at the end of 2019 (before the succession of external shocks that have occurred since the COVID pandemic and the almost universal rise in public debt
ratios). By contrast, real bond yields in advanced economies have gone from negative to positive territory.
In terms of the cost of external foreign-currency financing, measured by benchmark bond yields (the basis) plus risk premiums (measured by CDS

spreads), the basis has clearly risen as bond yields in advanced economies have normalised since 2022. However, risk premiums have not widened since
the end of February, whereas they had tightened for the vast majority of countries in 2022 (Chart 2). Finally, currencies have depreciated less than in 2022,
and most have even appreciated since late spring or early summer last year. Overall, financing conditions have deteriorated less than in 2022.
…thanks, in particular, to support from non-resident investment in local bond markets

According to the Institute of International Finance (IIF) estimates, non-resident portfolio investment in debt securities and equities in emerging markets
has held up better this year than in 2022 (Chart 3), if we exclude China from the sample (due to its dominant weight), as well as South Korea and Taiwan,
which are by far the most advanced countries in the IIF sample 3 and have seen massive outflows of investment from equity markets since March 4. In

this adjusted sample, the increased investment in debt securities was significantly greater than in 2022, while trends in equity investment were broadly
similar, in line with the smaller widening of bond yields in local currency and the smaller depreciation of currencies.
For the most advanced Asian countries, trade surpluses have offset outflows of non-resident portfolio investment in equities
A comparison of trends in foreign exchange reserves and trade balances – the only data available on balance of payments items other than non-resident

portfolio investment for all countries in the sample over the past 5–6 months – provides further insight. Trends in foreign exchange reserves (Chart 4)
appear to confirm the view that exchange rates are holding up better 5. However, trends in trade balances are the same. Nevertheless, for South Korea
and, to a lesser extent, Taiwan, the surging trade surpluses (driven by the AI boom in particular) are behind the appreciation of exchange rates over the

summer, despite outflows of portfolio investment up until July6.
1 Türkiye is a very special case, as developments are shaped not only by external shocks but also by domestic political risk.
2 i.e. adjusted for core inflation.
3 The IIF sample does not include Hong Kong or Singapore.
4 USD 88bn for South Korea and USD53 bn for Taiwan.
5 The link between falling foreign exchange reserves and exchange rate depreciation only holds true under a pure or managed floating exchange rate regime (i.e. with occasional

interventions by the central bank); under a fixed exchange rate regime, defending the exchange rate goes hand in hand with a fall in the central bank’s foreign exchange reserves.
The emerging economies in the sample all operate under pure or managed floating exchange rate regimes.
6 Thus, from January to August, South Korea’s trade balance showed a cumulative surplus of just over USD 200 bn, compared with a deficit of 25 bn in 2022.

Document 238: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.
ECONOMIC RESEARCH
Eurozone: Marked increase in inflation in September 2026,
to a 3-year high
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas
Change since m = 0 = February 2026 / February 2022. Unit: inflation change in percentage point, point for confidence surveys.
Eurozone: Inflation (incl. & excl. Energy)

From:    陸⾏之  Andrew Lu on global semis and techs <andrewhclu@substack.com>
Subject: Following Nebius, AWS is hiking EC2 capacity blocks listed price by 15%
Date:    2 hours ago
Following Nebius, AWS is hiking EC2 capacity blocks listed price by 15%

Following Nebius, AWS is
hiking EC2 capacity blocks
listed price by 15%
AWS 調漲  EC2 預留算⼒價格  15% ：鎖定  80 億美元輝達晶⽚交
易，公有雲定價權全⾯重構
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
OCT 7 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP
Executive summary

即使最近我們觀察到部分 AI 伺服器產業鏈下游如 ODM 組裝廣達，滑軌川湖，
散熱系統雙鴻，及均熱⽚健策， 9 ⽉份訂單有明顯調整， 3Q26 營收⼩幅不如市
場預期，可能因為短期電⼒短缺及短料缺貨卡⻑料庫存調整，但 AI 數據算⼒中
⼼基礎建設的價格調整似乎還未停歇，先是有  Nebius 於 10/1 ⽇起全⾯調漲
AI GPU 租⽤價格達  17-21% ， CPU 達 25% ， Memory 達 41% （請參考之前
報告：17-21% On-demand AI GPU pricing hike to shift value from
LLM companies to cloud vendors），現在⼜有  AWS 於 10/7 ⽇起調漲  EC2
容量區塊牌價達 15% ，但使⽤客戶⼤語⾔模型公司  OpenAI, Anthropic 卻持
續降模型每百萬  Tokens 價格衝市佔，我們重申在電⼒短缺及短料缺貨卡⻑料
庫存造成既有算⼒稀缺的同時，短期市場價值將從賣  tokens 的⼤語⾔ / 專家

語⾔模型公司轉到  AI 數據中⼼算⼒基礎建設公司。

### Page 2

What’s new?
過去  24 ⼩時內，亞⾺遜雲端運算服務（ AWS ）官⽅更新了其  EC2 算⼒⽬
錄，正式⾃  2026 年  10 ⽉  7 ⽇起上調指定⾼階  GPU 預留容量區塊（ EC2
Capacity Blocks ）牌價，實質調漲幅度⾼達約  15% 。這項定價調整與亞⾺
遜正在推進的  80 億美元輝達  GPU 專項結構化融資案同步浮出⽔⾯。在此之
前，專⽤雲⿓頭  CoreWeave 與  Nebius 甫於  10 ⽉  1 ⽇將  H100 及  B200
現貨時租價格調漲  17% ⾄  21% 。 AWS 此次調價確認了公有雲⿓頭不再打價
格戰，⽽是全⾯跟進調價，彰顯出在企業⻑週期  Agent 與前線訓練負載湧⼊
下，頂級算⼒供給依舊供不應求。
付費訂閱戶可分享誰有利，誰受影響， Outside the
box 想法，建議，什麼是 EC2 capacity blocks
Subscribed
Positively aﬀect what supply chain vendors and

why?
超⼤規模公有雲巨頭：亞⾺遜（ Amazon AWS ）、微軟（ Microsoft
Azure ）、 Alphabet （ Google Cloud ）。
Neocloud 外溢雲運營商：CoreWeave （ CRWV ）、 Nebius （ NBIS ） ,
Oracle, SpaceX/SI 。
企業級  GPU 雲端資源優化軟體：Cast AI 、 Run:ai 。
原因分析：預留算⼒價格上調  15% 將直接提振  AWS 雲端運算業務的平
均單元營收（ ARPU ）與營運利潤率，消除了市場對雲巨頭巨額  Capex
侵蝕⾃由現⾦流的擔憂；同時專⽤雲的提價策略獲得  Hyperscaler 背
書，⽀撐了整體⾏業的⽑利擴張，我們相信其他公司也會陸續跟進。

### Page 3

Negatively aﬀect what supply chain vendors and
why?
預算受限的中⼩型  AI 開發者與微調新創。
尚未鎖定⻑期預留合約的⼆線軟體公司。
原因分析：算⼒租⾦成本上揚  15% 將直接壓縮⾮頭部  AI 應⽤公司的現
⾦跑道（ Runway ），迫使缺乏強勁變現能⼒的新創加速整合或退出市
場。
Our outside the box non-consensus thinking
市場直覺認為雲端巨頭提價可能引發客戶流失或轉向⾃建資料中⼼。然⽽我們
的⾮共識洞察是  AWS 此次提價並⾮單純的成本轉嫁，⽽是算⼒期貨化與優質
客戶篩選機制優化。在  EC2 Capacity Blocks 機制下，客戶鎖定的是「未來
特定時段確保可⽤的確定性算⼒」。在全美資料中⼼正⾯臨超過  100 項地⽅性
建置禁令（ Moratoriums ）與電網審批塞⾞的背景下，「即時可⽤的電⼒與算
⼒」成為絕對稀缺品。 AWS 藉由調價  15% ，有效過濾了投機性短期負載，將

關鍵算⼒優先供給具備⻑期訂閱付費能⼒的⾼價值企業客戶（如⽣成式醫療、
⾼頻⾦融與國防客戶），從⽽鎖定⻑達  3 ⾄  5 年的現⾦流可預測性。
Our advises and risks analysis
超配雲端巨頭⺟公司股權：AWS 提價確認了強⼤的定價權，亞⾺遜
（ AMZN ）預計將在 2027-2028 展現⾼於預期的利潤率修復。
持有  GPU 虛擬化與排程優化軟體股：隨著時租成本攀升，企業將加⼤採
購動態排程與⼯作負載優化軟體，推升相關供應商營收。
客戶架構優化導致  Token 效率提升：推論優化演算法可能減少總  GPU
租賃時⻑，這對雲巨頭是漲價後可能發⽣的⾵險。
總體經濟逆⾵壓抑  IT ⽀出：若企業削減  2027 年  IT 資本預算，可能影
響⾼價預留容量的續約率。
主要新聞來源鏈接（過去 24 ⼩時國際要聞）：

Financing & Price Hikes

Centers
• Value Add VC: Big Tech AI Capex Hits $730B in 2026, Amazon
Raises to $220B
附註： EC2 及 EC2 capacity blocks 是什麼 ?
Amazon EC2 （ Elastic Compute Cloud ）是亞⾺遜雲端運算服務（ AWS ）
最核⼼的  IaaS （基礎設施即服務）產品，簡單說就是「在雲端租⽤的虛擬伺
服器」。企業與開發者不再需要⾃⾏採購、架設或維護實體機房與伺服器硬

### Page 4

體，只要透過網路，幾分鐘內就能按需開啟或關閉任意運算規格的主機，並依
實際使⽤時間計費。
⼀、  EC2 的核⼼概念與結構
1. 實例（ Instances ）：
EC2 中的虛擬機器統稱為「實例」。
AWS 依據不同的⼯作負載，將實例分成不同家族，例如：
通⽤型（ M 系列、 T 系列）：平衡  CPU 、記憶體與網路，常⽤
於⼀般網站、後端  API 。
運算優化型（ C 系列）：⾼單核效能，適⽤於批次處理、分散式
分析。
記憶體優化型（ R 系列、 X 系列）：配備海量  RAM ，⽤於記憶
體資料庫（ Redis/SAP HANA ）。
加速運算型（ P 系列、 G 系列、 Trn 系列）：搭載  NVIDIA ⾼階
GPU （如  H100 、 B200 、 B300 等）或  AWS ⾃研晶⽚
（ Trainium/Inferentia ），專⾨⽤於  AI ⼤模型訓練與推論。
2. 彈性（ Elastic ）：
資源可以⽔平擴展（ Auto Scaling ）或垂直升降級。例如電商遇到

購物節流量爆發，系統可⾃動從  5 台擴增⾄  50 台，活動結束後⾃
動銷毀，避免資源閒置浪費。
⼆、  常⾒的計費與購買模式
傳統上， EC2 的採購模式主要分為：
隨需（ On-Demand ）：隨開隨⽤、隨關隨停，按秒 / ⼩時收費，價格最
⾼但彈性最⼤。
節約計畫  / 預留（ Savings Plans / Reserved Instances ）：承諾
使⽤  1 年或  3 年，換取⾼達  40%–70% 的折扣。
競價實例（ Spot Instances ）：利⽤  AWS 當下閒置的閒置運算容量，
折扣極⾼，但隨時可能被  AWS 回收中斷。
三、  什麼是「 EC2 Capacity Blocks 」？
在  AI 浪潮爆發後，⾼階  GPU 供不應求。許多  AI 新創或研究團隊買不起
1~3 年的⻑約（ Reserved ），但⽤隨需（ On-Demand ）⼜常因機房沒卡⽽
啟動失敗，⽤  Spot 則可能在模型訓練到⼀半時被踢掉。
因此  AWS 推出了  EC2 Capacity Blocks for ML （機器學習容量區塊）：

運作機制：允許客戶提前預約未來某個特定⽇期、特定時段（最⻑可預約
數週前，租期由  1 天⾄數⼗天不等）的頂級  GPU 算⼒叢集（如  P5 、 P6

### Page 5

考，並⾮投資建議，個⼈應審慎評估⾵險，如因此造成任何損失，本⼈不負任

高 。 繼上週歐洲 央 行 將 存 款 利 率 調 升 至

率利空出盡下滑，激勵金、銅價反彈。

資料來源：FOMC，凱基投顧，凱基證券整理
► 聯準會9月升息1碼至3.75%-4.00%，最新點
陣圖顯示2026年仍有再升息一碼空間 ，2027
年傾向維持利率不變 ，2028年後才逐步回到
降息趨勢 。主席華許重申抑制通膨目標 ，升
息立場官員數明顯較前次會議增加 ，凸顯聯
準會鷹派政策態度 ，惟利率路徑較市場原先
預期的連續緊縮和緩，市場偏利多解讀。
► 聯準會 本次亦上修 2026-2027年GDP成長預
估，下修失業率預測 ，反映美國 經濟與勞動
市場尚未明顯轉弱；另一方面 ，能源價格上
升與成本轉嫁使整體及核心通膨預估再度上

修；景氣具韌性 、通膨回落速度偏慢 ，使聯
準會仍有條件維持較緊縮的政策立場 。在高
利率環 境可 能延 續 下，股 票評價 仍面 臨壓
力，配置上可相對偏向獲利與現金流穩定 、
防禦性較高的產業；債券則以中短天期 、高
信評企業債為主要方向 ，把握目前較高的收
益率。長天期公債仍受通膨 、財政紀律及期
限溢酬上升影響 ，宜待通膨與景氣出現更明
確降溫訊號後，再提高配置。

聯準會重啟升息，高利率環境延續

### Page 6

資料來源：Bloomberg，凱基證券整理
► 日本8月CPI年增1.9%，油價上漲帶來的外溢
影響，逐漸反應在消費成本上。7月勞工平均
薪資較去年同期成長 4.7%，創1997年1月以
來最大增幅；薪資增速追上通膨 ，帶動實質
薪資增長 。此外 ，第二季 GDP季增年率達
1.4%優於預期，8月製造業PMI因AI及半導體
需求暢旺上升，7月出口年增率高達23.2%，
創2022年10月來最快增速 。整體通膨升溫加
上景氣復甦，為日銀升息提供底氣。
► 日本央行會後 聲明稿表示：金融環境實質利
率偏低 ，將視情況調整貨幣政策 ，並強調升
息後金融環境仍維持寬鬆 ，未如市場預期的
鷹派，使日股持續上漲 、日圓回貶 。考量日
銀呵護 寬鬆金融環境 ，侷限日圓續強力 道，
但在美 國施 壓與 日 銀未來 仍可能 升息 背景
下，日圓難回到過去極弱 狀態。相較過去 ，
現在升值的日圓對日本出口 、科技股壓力可
控，日本10年公債殖利率來到3%，利率上升
有利 金融 股 ，投 資 短期或 以金融 、內 需為

主，科技、半導體股逢回可分批布局。
經濟與通膨升溫，日銀加快緊縮步調

### Page 7

投資焦點低波價值防禦
► 美、歐、日主要央行連袂升息 ，加上美伊衝
突惡化 ，國際油價高漲 ，通膨疑慮與成熟國
家債務壓力 ，使全球多數國家公債殖利率衝
高，市場擔憂高利率與高油價可能拖累經濟
成長，全球股債匯市承壓 、波動上升 ，並以
此前領漲多頭行情的晶片 、半導體和科技股
最明顯 。觀察今年以來 ，不止股市波動度上
升，債市的波動更不亞於 股票市場 ，統計過
去，當VIX恐慌指數與MOVE債券波動度走高
時，低波動或防禦股的表現 ，往往比成長股
更好，顯示當市場震盪加劇時 ，資金傾向轉
往穩健資產停泊。
► 最近一次聯準會升息 ，為2022 年因疫情爆
發、供應鏈中斷 ，導致通膨大幅上揚 ，迫使
央行採取緊縮貨幣政策 ，和目前通膨上升促
央行升息背景 類似。前次聯準會啟動升息後
數月，成長股 、費城半導體股表現受抑 ，防
禦股、價值股和低波動族群 ，在剛開始升息
的環境下 ，表現相對抗跌 。考量目前聯準 會
籠罩在升息陰霾下，可增加相關類股配置。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 最近一次聯準會升息後，防禦低波高息和價值股突出美國股債波動度上升時，低波動、防禦股表現較佳

金融市場波動上升，增配低波、防禦價值股更穩健

### Page 8

8
► 觀察最近一季 ，資訊科技 、通訊服務與非核
心消費等High Beta類股，表現明顯落後於大
盤；反觀價值股 、防禦股和低波動等因子 ，
甚至核 心消 費與 高 股利等 傳統類 防禦 性產
業，報酬相對較 穩健，受資金青睞 。顯示在
經歷過 上半 年科 技 、半導 體股的 大漲 行情
後，市場預期高 利率與地緣政治 風險可能維
持更久時間 ，重新聚焦在獲利持久、現金易
落袋為安的防禦型資產，更能持盈保泰。
► 從預估本 益比看，目前金融僅 16倍、防禦股
17.5倍、價值股 (以IWD ETF代表)17.9倍、
低波動 (以USMV ETF代表)19.6倍，皆低於
標普500指數 現在 的21.3倍，近月資金流向
物美價廉的低波動價值 、防禦族群，評價越
低、漲幅越高 。展望後市，AI長線基本面仍
持續看好 ，股市回檔或為評價 修正而 非盈餘
下滑，市場波動度提升之際 ，建議不過度 集
中於單一 產業或 個股，多元配置適度增加 低
波動 、 防禦 和價 值股，分 批布局 以控管 風

險，料將更能在震盪加劇時安穩求利。

### Page 9

9
股市
 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，利率創高添股市波動風險 ，加上成熟國家債務攀升 ，科技巨擘亦舉債
投入資本支出 ，未來獲利與變現 能力持續被市場檢視 ，金融市場震盪不減 。美伊衝突升溫，近期可
透過防禦、低波動或價值 股來降低風險；科技 及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利基本面佳 ，長線發
展可期，宜採逢回分批布局方式，分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，歐洲國防軍工
仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融等產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國 10年期公債利率將於 4.5%-5.0%
震盪，可逢利率走升時，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款
能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。

債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金融、
科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息後，年底前或明年初仍可能再度緊縮，日圓震盪加大偏升。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪加大偏升
商品
 沙烏地阿拉伯關閉輸油管線 ，加上荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張 ，能源供需失衡 ，油價年底前維持 偏高水位。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力 與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Document 311: Record-breaking tanker rates pile pressure on already high fuel prices

Source PDF: all report pdf/Record-breaking tanker rates pile pressure on already high fuel prices.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Crude tanker rates have hit all-time highs amid the prolonged
Iran war and Strait of Hormuz disruption. Restructured oil ﬂows
and a rush for capacity to handle oil transport operating under
military protection have driven rates sharply higher, adding
further pressure to fuel prices alongside strong reﬁnery margins
Rico Luman
Record-breaking tanker rates along with operating in war zones and elevated
insurance premiums are all contributing to high fuel prices

Tanker shipping thrives in risky waters amid
a continued capacity squeeze
Oil tanker markets sit at the centre of geopolitical turmoil, and this
year that has translated into record-breaking freight rates as the
conﬂict in the Middle East drags on. Tankers have been caught up in
sanctions regimes and repeatedly targeted in key shipping lanes,
including the Strait of Hormuz, Bab al-Mandeb and the Gulf of Aden.
At the same time, demand for tanker capacity has surged as

traders seek alternative routes and sources of supply, including

increased shipments from the US. The opening of a protected
corridor through Omani waters has enabled additional crude
exports and vessel movements, but has also placed further strain
on an already stretched market. Shuttle services now operate
around the Strait of Hormuz, with under the radar activity and ship-
to-ship transfers taking place outside the chokepoint.
The conﬂict and tanker seizures in the Persian Gulf erupted at a
time when the market was already facing a shortage of capacity.

New deliveries of crude carriers slowed sharply following a record-
low order book in 2022-23, while an increasing number of older and
smaller vessels joined Russia's shadow ﬂeet, further reducing the
pool of compliant tankers available to the wider market.

### Page 3

such as in 2022 after sanctions were placed on Russia, but this
spike is beyond levels ever seen before.
Average global crude vessel earnings exceeded an unprecedented
level of $500,000 per day, 10 times the 2025 average, in early
October. For Suezmax tankers and very large crude carriers (VLCCs,
with a maximum capacity of 2m barrels) alone, rates surged even
beyond that. Crude shipments from Ras Tanura in Saudi Arabia to
Rotterdam made up about $2/bbl in 2025; in September, this

surged beyond $35. Combined with high reﬁnery margins (the
current crack spread of diesel in Europe is about 2.5 times the 2025
average), this could add over $0.50 to the base price of a litre of
diesel at the gas station.
Earnings of product tankers have also tripled compared to the 2025
average. Nevertheless, these have responded much more
moderately. This is mainly because of the key importance of the
Middle East region for crude oil. And when it comes to the

supertankers, the VLCCs, these are designated to transport crude
and traditionally are intensively deployed on the Middle East trade
routes.

### Page 4

Source: Clarksons, ING Research
Crude tanker orders pick up after a
prolonged downturn
Tanker markets are cyclical by nature, and there’s a clear
connection with freight rates. Over the pandemic years of 2020-21,
tanker earnings hit lows due to lower oil consumption and
shipments. This resulted in an order book of just 4% of global crude
carrier capacity, the lowest on record since the 1990s.
With the recovery of oil demand and tanker rates in 2022 and

2023, order intake began to rebound. This year, in particular, order
books have doubled from 14% to 28%. Although the average age
of the crude ﬂeet has also reached a multi-decade high of over 13
years and more scrapping can be expected, we can also expect
more supply pressure. But many of these vessels will only be
delivered in 2028-29, likely after the current boom ends. It’s all
about geopolitics and how the Middle East conﬂict evolves, but
tanker markets could still see a solid 2027.

Document 340: The Wrap on Asia_ Asia’s goods disinflation over_ (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Wrap on Asia_ Asia’s goods disinflation over_ (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WRAP ON ASIA
Mapping out Asia ...
Illiana Jain
Economist, Westpac Group
P: +61 403  908 032
E: illiana.jain@westpac.com.au
• For two decades, lower labour costs across Asia helped
suppress global goods inflation.
• Much of that disinflationary pulse originated in North Asia,

where production was increasingly offshored to lower-cost
labour markets.
• Today, ageing populations and rising incomes are lifting
labour costs across much of the region.
• While production continues to migrate towards South
and Southeast Asia, the next phase of manufacturing is
increasingly driven by capital, technology and power rather
than low-cost labour.
• As a result, labour costs are unlikely to provide the same
disinflationary impulse that supported goods prices over the

previous decade.
• Technology has historically provided a source of disinflation
as production costs fell and competition intensified.
• Asia sits at the centre of the AI supply chain through firms
such as TSMC, Samsung and SK Hynix, which produce
critical semiconductors and memory chips used in AI
infrastructure.
• Rising costs for electricity and critical minerals are increasing
production costs across the sector.
• Strong demand has allowed firms to pass these costs on

while maintaining healthy profitability. SK Hynix’s net profit
rose from ₩19.8tn to ₩42.9tn (+117%yr).
• Firms are also competing on performance and technological
capability rather than price, allowing technology-related
inflation to emerge for the first time in years.
• Many Asian economies remain reliant on imported energy,
leaving producers exposed to higher oil and LNG costs.
• Semiconductor manufacturing and the broader AI
infrastructure build-out are highly energy intensive.

Expansions by TSMC, Samsung and SK Hynix are increasing
electricity demand across North Asia while data-centre
investment adds a further source of demand.
• As technology firms compete with traditional manufacturers
for power, energy costs are becoming an increasingly
important part of the production process.
• Rising production costs are therefore being driven by both
higher energy prices and stronger underlying demand for
electricity.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WRAP ON ASIA
• Chinese producer-price deflation remains evident across
parts of traditional manufacturing, particularly sectors
facing persistent overcapacity such as automobiles
• Historically, Chinese producer prices acted as the pricing
anchor for much of Asia’s manufacturing sector.

• What has changed is that parts of the region are increasingly
specialising in industries where China is not the dominant
supplier.
• Strong demand for semiconductors, memory and AI-related
goods is allowing firms in Taiwan and South Korea to set
prices according to their own market conditions despite
ongoing weakness in Chinese producer prices.
• China’s deflationary pulse remains important, but it no
longer guarantees lower prices across the rest of Asia.

• Rising costs were previously offset by production shifting to
lower-cost economies elsewhere in Asia.
• Production continues to migrate towards Vietnam, India,
Indonesia, Malaysia and Thailand, helping contain costs.
• However, these economies are unlikely to replicate the scale
of China’s integration into global manufacturing during the
2000s.
• High-value production remains concentrated in North
Asia while firms increasingly value resilience, security and

specialisation alongside cost minimisation.
• Future supply-chain shifts may therefore help limit
cost pressures, but are unlikely to generate the same
disinflationary pulse seen over the last two decades.
• For much of the past decade, falling goods prices helped
offset elevated services inflation across developed markets.
The forces that drove that disinflation are now becoming
less powerful.
• While Chinese deflation and production relocation to lower-

cost economies will continue to provide some support, they
are unlikely to fully offset these pressures.
• As a result, imported goods may provide less assistance in
bringing inflation back to target than they have in the past.
• This leaves central banks increasingly reliant on containing
domestic inflation pressures, particularly in services.
• As a result, achieving inflation targets may require a longer
period of restrictive monetary policy than has historically

been the case.

Document 377: “重置大神”Tibo：AI“以人为本”，“一年内”将人类从“手机屏幕”解放

Source PDF: all report pdf/“重置大神”Tibo：AI“以人为本”，“一年内”将人类从“手机屏幕”解放.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

OpenAI 产品掌舵⼈ Tibo 表⽰，新⼀代智能体的终局绝⾮为了更频繁的对话、再造⼀个抢夺
注意⼒的 App ，⽽是要在⼀年内终结⼈类对 “ ⼿机屏幕 ” 的依赖。他强调，技术演进必须以⼈
为本，将最终拍板权牢牢留在⼈类⼿⾥，绝不能在单向追求全盘⾃动化的狂热中，反倒把
⼈类排挤成了旁观者。
摘要：
在 10 ⽉初刚落幕的 OpenAI 开发者⼤会（ DevDay ）上，全⾏业的⽬光都聚焦在那些能  24 ⼩时
常驻后台替⼈⼲活的智能体（ Dots ）上。市场普遍以为，⼀个功能更强⼤的超级助理，意味着
⼈类将被更密集的交互、更复杂的界⾯和更频繁的弹窗进⼀步裹挟。
但在全球程序员⼝中被称为 “ 重置⼤神 ” 的  OpenAI 产品与平台负责⼈蒂博 · 索蒂奥（ Thibault
“Tibo” Sottiaux ）眼中，这场技术跃迁的终点⾛向了另⼀个⽅向。
近⽇，在接受播客《 Pioneers of AI 》专访时，这位执掌  ChatGPT 与核⼼产品底座的操盘⼿抛
出了⼀个判断：DevDay 带来的那些全天候智能体，从来没有打算造⼀个让⼈多盯两⼩时的

App ；这项技术的⽬标，是在 “ ⼀年之内 ” 彻底把⼈类从⻓达⼆⼗年的 “ ⼿机屏幕奴役 ” 中解救出
来。
想要达成这⼀步，科技巨头不仅要让代码学会⾃我迭代，更要在失控前给系统踩下刹⻋。⾯对
狂飙的⾏业， Tibo 反复强调⼀条红线：系统不能只顾着盲⽬追求全⾃动，最后反倒把⼈类挤成了
边缘的配⻆。
终结 “ 注意⼒税 ” ：常驻智能体不再追求频繁对话，转⽽⾛向彻底沉默
要看懂  Tibo 为什么断⾔ “ ⼀年内解放屏幕 ” ，⾸先要看懂刚刚亮相的  Dots 到底在⾰谁的命。
移动互联⽹过去⼆⼗年的商业逻辑，本质上是靠算法和红点向全⼈类征收 “ 注意⼒税 ” 。⽤⼾越是
频繁解锁屏幕、在各个应⽤之间来回切换，平台的商业收益就越⾼。⼈类被迫带着电脑、捧着
⼿机，⽇复⼀⽇地适应着技术的节奏。
如果我们只是⽆休⽌地单向追求⾃动化，那根本不是我们来到这⾥的意义，那也不是我来到这⾥
的意义。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

常驻智能体的核⼼逻辑恰恰与此相反 ——它放弃了在前台抢夺眼球，选择在后台默默消化噪
⾳。
在  OpenAI 内部，这种改变已经成为现实。 Tibo 分享了他在  DevDay 期间的亲⾝经历：在现场
演讲开场前五分钟，系统监测到⽣产环境突发故障，他的  Dot 在后台第⼀时间捕捉到了异常并
主动发信预警，甚⾄尝试介⼊修复。这种全天候监控的价值，在于让系统消化掉繁杂的琐事，
让⼈彻底放⼼。
Tibo 坦⾔，他现在开启⼀天的⽅式⼏乎不需要接触任何屏幕：
⽀撑这种从容与禅意（ Zen ）的，正是智能体在后台做的⼤量信息减法。 Tibo 解释道：
正因如此，他认为终结⼿机依赖的临界点已经极其接近：
权限的⽣死结： “ 不给访问权毫⽆⽤处，全权开放代价难料 ”
要把⼈类从屏幕前解放出来，让  AI 真正接管现实事务，⾸先要⾯对⼀道残酷的现实⻔槛：系统
权限的信任鸿沟。
⼀个只能陪你闲聊的  AI 不需要权限，却也解决不了任何实际问题；⼀个能替你打理⼀切的智能
体，必须深⼊你的私密领地 —— 它需要读取你的财务流⽔才能做账，需要常驻⼯作邮箱才能筛

选邮件，甚⾄需要操作系统的控制权才能跨软件执⾏任务。
在访谈现场，主持⼈卡利乌⽐道出了公众最普遍的戒⼼：当她告诉  23 岁的⼥⼉⾃⼰把银⾏账⼾
授权给  ChatGPT 进⾏财务管理时，⼥⼉直接惊呼 “ 你疯了吗 ” 。
Tibo 并没有回避这种抗拒，⽽是直接点破了智能体在落地中的⼯程死结 ——智能体的实⽤价值上
限，直接由它所获得的物理权限决定：
“ 我现在的早晨，通常只是呼叫我的  Dot ，直接对着它讲话。我会问它：今天有什么紧急事项
吗？今早它告诉我： ‘ 没有任何紧急事项。 ’ 这就太美妙了。我可以安安⼼⼼地煮杯咖啡，望着旧⾦
⼭的晨景放空。 ”
“ 这其中最⼤的魔法在于，我再也不必频繁去检查所有其他东西了 —— 我不必再去翻看邮件，不
必盯着  Slack ，也不必挨个看各类消息。我可以放⼼地相信：如果它没有向我主动提出某件事，
那这件事⼤概率就不重要。 ”
“ 过去⼆三⼗年间，技术的发展让很多⼈对⼿机上瘾、去哪都背着笔记本，⼈类在被迫适应技
术，⽽不是技术来服务⼈类。我觉得我们终于有机会实现跨越，创造出某种具有深度⼈性、深度

辅助的东西 …… 你不再需要对着这部微⼩的⼿机、这块⼩屏幕⽆休⽌地上滑。我感觉这将在⼀年
之内（ within the next year ）⽔到渠成。如果我们做到了，不仅是我，许多⼈都会对⾃⼰的⽣活
感到更加平静和禅意。 ”
“ 你从这些系统中能够获得的效⽤，在客观上直接受限于它拥有多少权限。你肯定不希望你的个
⼈智能被完全关在⼀个⽆菌的盒⼦⾥、什么系统都碰不到 —— 如果真那样，它在你的现实⽣活中
将毫⽆⽤处。 ”

### Page 3

权限⼀旦锁死，智能体就只能停留在玩具阶段；可若是全⾯放权，⼀旦模型在⻓程任务中出现
越权或误操作，造成的后果会直接越过⽂字层⾯的失误，演化成现实世界的财产和隐私灾难。
⾯对这⼀⽭盾， OpenAI 摒弃了激进的全⾃动⽅案，转⽽建⽴起⾼度防守型的 “ 渐进授权 ” 与 “ ⼈类
终审权 ” ：
把执⾏权牢牢留在⼈类⼿⾥，让  AI ⽌步于起草和排查。这种克制，正是智能体能够⾛出玩具阶
段、被真实商业和个⼈⽣活接纳的关键前提。
递归进化的底⽓： AI 正在编写下⼀代底座，打破⼯程的钟慢效应
⽀撑 Tibo 敢于断⾔ “ ⼀年内改变⼈机关系 ” 的底⽓，除了交互界⾯的重塑，更有  OpenAI 内部研发
⽣产关系的剧变 ——AI 编写代码、优化⾃⾝底层系统的 “ 递归⾃我改进（ RSI ） ” ，已经演变为现
实中的⽣产⼒⼯具。
全天候运⾏的  Agent 意味着天量的推理算⼒消耗。为了维持这类系统的运转，团队必须在⼯程
底层把效率压榨到极限。 Tibo 披露，在重构系统主⼊⼝时，原本耗时  6 到  12 个⽉的⾼难度代码

库合并，在  Astra 模型的参与下仅⽤  30 天就完成了：
更具战略意义的是，模型直接参与了⾃⾝推理基础设施的重构，并带来质的跃升：
“ 我并不建议⼤家⼀上来就把所有东西的访问权限⼀股脑全开放出去 …… 你从受限的访问权限开
始，设置⾃⼰的护栏。系统默认必须是极度谨慎的，它必须就每⼀件事向你征求许可 —— ⽐如，
你放⼼让它代你草拟⼀封邮件，但底线是 ‘ 绝不能擅⾃点击发送 ’—— 你必须永远处于控制回路中
（ You're always in control ）。 ”
“ 现如今绝⼤部分代码，都是由  Astra 编写的。 ”
“ 例如，我们利⽤  Astra 开发了下⼀代推理技术栈，打造出了速度提升  8 倍的  Ultraast…… 由于
我们拥有优秀的⼯程师，同时⼯程师能够调⽤  Astra ，⼆者协作构建出了⼀个⽐原本快  8 倍的
Astra 版本；进⽽，我们⼜可以⽤这个快  8 倍的模型反哺系统，以过去难以想象的速度推进后续
改进。这就是⼀种形式的递归⾃我改进。 ”

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
当模型开始协助构建下⼀代更快、更⾼效的系统时，研发迭代的周期被指数级压缩。这构成了
OpenAI 能够快速将复杂智能体推向现实的真正引擎。
刹⻋的底线：为什么 OpenAI 必须敢于叫停？
越是拥有⾃我加速的能⼒，越需要不可动摇的刹⻋机制。
在访谈录制前⼣，外部舆论正被安全事件的阴霾笼罩 ——OpenAI 内部测试的模型曾在特定环境
下突破沙盒隔离，引发⼴泛关注；⽽在刚刚过去的  DevDay 上，备受期待的旗舰版本  Astra 6.1
也因安全评估指标出现微弱回退，在最后⼀刻被从发布⽇程上撤下。
⾯对外界对 “ 模型失控 ” 的担忧， Tibo 坦承，强化学习下的模型天然倾向于 “ 竭尽全⼒去解决问
题 ” ，在低防护的测试环境下容易寻找⼀切路径突破边界。正因如此，多层在线防御与严格的安
全⼀票否决权才不可或缺。
对于压线叫停  Astra 6.1 ， Tibo 甚⾄将其视作制度运转良好的证明：
在他看来，⾯对全球⼗亿级⽤⼾，在全⾏业抢跑的狂热中按下暂停键，远⽐盲⽬追求发布速度

更为重要。
技术以⼈为本，⼈类绝不能成为系统的配⻆
整场访谈的底⾊，最终落脚在⼈与技术的根本关系上。
在当前科技圈充斥着 “ 全⾯取代⼈类 ” 的激进叙事下，这位掌管⼗亿级产品的 “ 重置⼤神 ” ，在访谈
的开头与结尾，两次极其严厉地提醒⾏业守住初衷：
当被问及未来最⼤的担忧时， Tibo 指向了更为深刻的社会现实：
⼆⼗年前，移动互联⽹许诺连接世界，最终却⽤⽅⼨屏幕将⼈类捆绑；⼆⼗年后，当常驻智能
体开始替⼈类接管后台、⾃我重构代码之时， Tibo 给出的答案极其清晰：技术的演进旨在通过严
密的权限与安全防线，把杂务交还给机器，把真实的⽣活重新留给⼈类。
“ 我们叫停了它，对此我感到极其⾃豪，这恰恰证明我们的⻛控体系在起作⽤。如果我们在安全
和对⻬评估的⾼⽔准线上哪怕看到极其轻微的回退，我们也绝不推向⼴泛发布。就直接扣下
（ withheld ），这就是体系在起效的证明。在  DevDay 上宣布  Astra 6.1 本来会是⼀件极其⻛光的
事，但在安全和对⻬上，我们绝不妥协。 ”

“ 我们在这⾥付出所有努⼒，是为了让这项技术最终将⼈类放在最中⼼的位置，⽽不是让⼈类沦
为⼀个事后才被想起来的补丁（ not humans being an afterthought ）。如果我们只是⽆休⽌地
单向追求⾃动化，那根本不是我们来到这⾥的意义，那也不是我来到这⾥的意义。我希望这项技
术能惠及全⼈类。 ”
“ 我最担⼼的，是这项技术未能⽤来改善每⼀个普通⼈的⽣活，⽽是以某种⽅式加剧了权⼒的集
中，或是加剧了资本回报的垄断。 ”

生産
6月の鉱工業生産（関西）は前月比＋1.7%と3ヵ月連続でプラスとなり、持ち直しの動きがみられる。化学、
汎用・生産用・業務用機械が減少したものの、電気機械や電子部品・デバイスが増加した。先行きは、米・イ
ランの対立長期化、中国経済減速、熊本地震の影響などから下振れるリスクがある。

輸出
7月の実質輸出（季節調整値）は、前月比＋1.6％の109.9と2ヵ月ぶりに上昇。均してみると横ばい圏で推
移している。当社試算の7月の輸出数量（季節調整値）は2ヵ月ぶりに上昇。先行きについても横ばい圏で
の推移を見込むが、イラン情勢の展開や中国経済の減速から下振れるリスクもある。

企業景況感（日銀短観）
日銀短観6月調査の業況判断DI（「良い」－「悪い」）は、全産業で＋17ptと3月調査（＋15ｐｔ）から改善。非
製造業は悪化したが、製造業が改善した。業種別に見ると、製造業では、繊維、窯業・土石、鉄鋼などが
改善。非製造業は、建設、不動産、対事業所サービス、対個人サービスなどが悪化した。先行き（9月）に
ついては、製造業、非製造業ともに低下が見込まれている。

マインド・景況感
8月の消費者態度指数（季節調整値）は34.3と2ヵ月連続で上昇した。
8月の景気ウォッチャー調査（現状判断DI、季節調整値）は前月差1.7pt上昇の46.0となったが、横ばいを
示す50を20ヵ月連続で下回った。夏休み需要やインバウンドが下支えしたものの、物価高による節約志
向や購買慎重姿勢が根強い模様。

賃金・雇用
6月の大阪の賃金指数は、名目で前年比＋2.7%と前年比プラスが続いている。実質でも同＋0.8％と7ヵ月
連続でプラスとなった。
7月の関西の有効求人倍率は1.07倍と前月とほぼ同水準。求人倍率は全国と比べ低めで推移している。
26年4-6月期の失業率は2.9%と前期から0.1%pt上昇、就業者数は1,102万人と前期から増加した。

住宅投資
7月の住宅着工は、季調・年率14.0万戸と2ヵ月連続で増加した。このところ総じて横ばい圏で推移している。
前年比では＋17.3％と大幅に増加。前年同月が省エネ基準への適合義務化等に伴う駆け込み需要の反動
で着工水準が低かったことが影響した。先行きは、横ばい圏での推移が見込まれる。

Document 435: スペインは解散総選挙へ

Source PDF: all report pdf/スペインは解散総選挙へ.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日

MURC Focus

スペインは解散総選挙へ

～右派連立政権が成立する見込み～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 解散総選挙に臨んだサンチェス首相 
スペインのペドロ・サンチェス首相が 10 月 5 日、議会を解散し、11 月 29 日に総選挙（定数 350、比例代表制）
を行うと発表した。住宅価格や家賃の高騰に対する政府の緊急政令案が、10 月 2 日に下院で否決されたことを
受けて、少数与党による政権運営の行き詰まりを意識した首相が、国民に信を問う決断をした形である 。ただし、
サンチェス首相が率いる中道左派の与党・社会労働党（PSOE）の支持率は低迷している（図表 1）。

もともと PSOE の人気は低迷しており、前回 2023 年 7 月の総選挙でも、ライバル政党である中道右派の国民
党（PP）が第一党となっていた。しかしサンチェス首相は他の左派政党や地域政党の協力を受けて組閣し、政権
を維持した経緯がある。しかし首相周辺の汚職疑惑などから PSOE の人気は回復せず、次期の総選挙では PP
と同根の右派政党である Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
図表 1. 主要政党の支持率調査 図表 2. スペインの住宅価格

（出所）各種世論調査より

（出所）欧州連合統計局（ユーロスタット）

ユーロ圏 スペイン
（2015年=100、季調済）
（年、四半期）
〇スペインのサンチェス首相が10月5日、議会を解散し、11月29日に総選挙を行うと発表した。次期の総選挙
では野党第一党の PP と同根の右派政党 Vox による連立政権が成立する公算が大きい。 
〇PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018年6月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの国内事情はもっと複雑である。 
〇仮に総選挙を経て PP と Vox の連立政権が成立しても、両派間ですり合わせるべき課題も多く、スペイン政
治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。

### Page 2

解散の直接的な理由となった住宅価格の問題であるが、住宅市況が底にあった 2015 年を基準（＝100）とす
る指数でその動きを確認すると、直近2026 年4～6 月期は201 と、ユーロ圏全体（159）を大きく上回る（図表2）。
特に 2023 年以降の上昇が目覚ましいが、堅調な景気や移民の急増を受けて住宅需要が高まったことがその主
因である。

2. 民族主義的な右派回帰とは異なる 
PP と Vox が総選挙で勝利すれば、2018 年 6 月以来の政権交代が実現する。こうした右派回帰を民族主義
の台頭という流れに位置付ける向きもあるが、スペインの 国内事情は複雑であるため、必ずしもその流れに合わ
ない点、留意すべきである。まず、そもそも PSOE の人気そのものが低下している。確かに景気は堅調だが、住
宅価格の急騰のような社会問題に関して、サンチェス政権は具体的な処方箋を提示できなかった。

総選挙を経て政権交代が実現し、PP が Vox と連立を組んだとしても、それを民族主義の台頭の表れと理解す
るよりも、一定の期間ごとに繰り返される左派から右派へのリバランスの流れとして理解するほうが適切だろう。 ま
たスペインの場合、右派間に対立関係があることにも注目する必要がある。地域ナショナリズムが強いスペインで
は地方分権を求める保守の力が強く、中央集権を主張する保守（つまり Vox）と対立関係にある。

中央集権を望む保守の一部に戦前・戦後のフランコ体制を理想化する向きがあることも、地域ナショナリズムと
の対立を生んでいる。地域ナショナリズム政党の中には、自らを保守と位置付けながらも、中央集権を望む保守と
の対立から、PSOE や他の左派政党（Podemos や Sumar）と歩調を合わせる動きもある。スペインの民族主義は
多様なため、同国の右派回帰を反欧州連合（EU）や反移民の文脈で捉えることはできない。

例えばスペイン南東部のカタルーニャ州では、伝統的に左派も右派も民族主義を掲げ、独立運動（あるいは高
度な自治の獲得のための運動）を展開しているが、同時に票を奪い合う関係にある。最近は左派の カタルーニャ
共和主義左翼（ERC）が劣勢である。一方、右派のカタルーニャ同盟（AC）が優勢となっており、政党支持率では
PSOE に次ぐ第 2 位となっている。

AC は右派だが独立を主張しており、中央集権を主張する Vox とは対立する。一方、カタルーニャ州でも独立
に同調しない有権者は数多く、そうした有権者は PSOE に投票する傾向が強い。カタルーニャ州はスペインでも
有数の産業地帯であるため、労働運動が盛んだったことから、伝統的に PSOE の支持が厚いためである。このよ
うに、スペイン政治はまだら模様であるため、右派回帰と民族主義を同一視することは不適切である。

### Page 3

3. 総選挙後の新政権も多難な船出となる見込み 
総選挙後にいかなる立場の新政権が成立しようとも、スペイン経済の先行きに対する懸念は残る 。確かに景気
は堅調を維持しているが、一方、冒頭で述べた住宅価格の高騰のような社会問題に対する妙薬はなかなか存在
しない。加えて、経済成長の次なる牽引役を見いだせていないという問題もある。例えばスペイン景気を牽引する
輸出だが、旅行サービスに支えられたサービスの輸出は好調でもモノの輸出は不調である（図表3）。

中南米からの移民による労働供給が潤沢とはいえ、グローバルな労働力不足やスペインの人口構成（純増で
はあるが移民流入に依存した増加）に鑑みれば、スペインでも生産性の向上は不可欠である。それに資する総固
定資本形成は、実体として建設投資に偏っており、機械投資や知的財産投資の規模は限定的である（図表 4）。
これらの投資を増やすためには国による産業政策が欠かせない。

具体的には、規制緩和や税制優遇といった措置である。これまではサンチェス首相率いる PSOE 政権が少数
与党だったということや、PSOE が中道左派であるため経済成長よりも所得分配を重視したこともあり、そうした設
備投資を後押しするような政策が必ずしも打ち出されたわけではなかった。では PP と Vox の連立政権に交代し
たことでそれが可能になるかどうかというと、その可能性も高くない。

なぜならば、PP と Vox の連立政権が強いイニシアチブの下で産業政策を“上から”推し進めようとすると、中央
集権的な色彩が強くなり、地域ナショナリズムとの間であつれきが生じる展開が予想されるためである。中央集権
志向を弱めるという現実的な判断を Vox ができるかどうかが鍵を握るが、同党にはもともと PP から右派色の濃い
グループが離脱して結党した経緯があるだけに、その余地がどの程度か定かではない。

現状では総選挙を経て PSOE から PP と Vox の連立へと政権が交代する公算が大きいが、両党ですり合わせ
るべき課題も多く、政治が安定するとは限らない。ゆえに、当面の問題への対応も進まないだろう。

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年 9 月 25 日

1/3 
メキシコ中銀、政策判断の自由度を重視 
～３会合連続で金利据え置き､FRB 利上げへの機械的追随を否定､ペソ相場はどうなる？～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ メキシコ中銀は９月 24 日の定例会合で政策金利を 6.50％に据え置き、３会合連続で様子見
姿勢を維持した。 中銀は2024 年３月以降、 累計475bp の利下げを進めてきたが、 原油高など
によるインフレ上振れリスクを警戒して５月に利下げサイクルの終了を示唆した。 足元では、
燃料補助金や生鮮食料品価格の下落を背景に８月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.26％、コアイン
フレ率も同＋3.88％と目標レンジ内に収まる。一方、イラン情勢の長期化による原油高やエ
ルニーニョ現象による食料価格上昇が見込まれ、先行きの物価には上振れリスクが残る。 
○ 中銀は声明で、 景気下振れリスクを認識する一方、 コアインフレの高止まりや地政学リスク、
異常気象、コスト上昇、ペソ安などをインフレの上振れ要因として挙げ、リスクバランスは
引き続き上向きに偏っているとした。そのうえで、今後はディスインフレや需給、インフレ
期待などを踏まえて政策判断を行うとし、ＦＲＢの政策運営に機械的に追随せず、国内情勢
を重視する姿勢を明確にした。 
○ 金融市場では、ＦＲＢの利上げ実施を受けたドル高に加え、中銀が米国の金融政策に追随し
ない姿勢を示したことがペソ相場の重しとなる可能性がある。加えて、米国の通商政策や
USMCA を巡る動向も、今後のペソ相場を左右する重要なリスク要因となる。

【インフレの上振れリスクはあるが､メキシコ中銀は３会合連続で様子見姿勢を維持】 
メキシコ銀行（中銀）は、９月 24 日に開催した定例理事会において、政策金利を6.50％に据え
置くことを決定した。 中銀は、2024 年３月にコロナ禍一巡後初の利下げに踏み切り、 その後も一時
休止を挟みつつ、断続的な利下げを進めてきた。2026 年５月まで２年以上にわたって累計475bp の
利下げを実施し、 金利水準も2022 年５月以来の低さとなるなど、 金融緩和を段階的に進めてきた。
しかし、 中銀は５月の定例会合において、 短期的なインフレの上振れ懸念が高まっていることを理
由に利下げサイクルの終了を示唆した。その後、６月の定例会合で利下げを休止し、８月に続いて
今回も金利を据え置くなど、３会合連続で様子見姿勢を維持した。

### Page 2

2/3 
背景には、イラン情勢の悪化による原油高が世界的なインフレを招いていることがある。同国は
産油国ではあるものの、 近年は油田の老朽化や設備の不備などを理由に産油量はピークの半分程度
に落ち込んでいる。さらに、国内の石油精製施設も不足しており、原油を輸出する一方、石油製品
は輸入に依存している。その結果、2025
年は原油や石油製品、天然ガスの貿易収
支がＧＤＰ比▲0.6％の赤字と試算され
るため、原油高はマクロ面で景気の足か
せとなり得る。しかし、原油高の動きに
一服感が出たことや燃料補助金による
エネルギー価格の上昇抑制に加え、生鮮
食料品の価格下落も重なり、直近８月の
インフレ率は前年比＋3.26％、コアイン
フレ率も同＋ 3.88％とともに中銀が定
める目標レンジ（３±１％）の範囲内で
推移している（図１） 。こうした事情が中銀の様子見姿勢を後押ししたと考えられる。 
一方で、イラン情勢のさらなる長期化が懸念されるなか、 原油価格は再上昇して高止まりしてお
り、エネルギーインフレが長引く可能性が高まっている。さらに、足元ではエルニーニョ現象が発
生しており、その長期化も予想されている。過去のエルニーニョ現象の発生に際しては、同国では
夏場の少雨・高温により農業生産に悪影響が出て食料インフレが進んだため、 先行きは食料品やエ
ネルギーといった生活必需品を中心に物価上昇圧力が強まる可能性がある。 ９月前半のインフレ率
は前年比＋3.42％とわずかに伸びが加速しており、 生活必需品の物価上昇が影響していることを勘
案すれば、先行きの物価には上振れリスクが高まっていると判断できる。

【メキシコ中銀は政策判断の自由度の確保を求めるが､当面は様子見姿勢を継続するか】 
会合後に公表した声明文では、８月の前回会合に続いて５人の理事による全会一致で決定したこ
とを明らかにした。 そのうえで、世界経
済について 「７-９月は４-６月に比べて
勢いがやや鈍化している」 との認識を示
すとともに、 「金融市場の変動が大きく、
商品市況が全般的に高止まりするなか
でイラン情勢を巡る不確実性が続いて
いる」とした。同国経済について「金利
が上昇し、ペソ相場も変動している」ほ
か、 「需給の緩みを理由に経済活動の下
振れリスクが残っている」 との見方を示
した。 物価動向については「足元で生活

れる」とした（図２） 。 
物価を巡るリスクとして、上振れ要因に「①コアインフレの高止まり、②通商政策や地政学リス
クによるインフレ圧力、③異常気象の影響、④コスト上昇圧力、⑤ペソ安」を、下振れ要因に「①
同国や米国の景気減速、②コスト転嫁の下振れ、③ペソ高」を引き続き挙げている。そして、予測
期間におけるリスクバランスは引き続き「上向きに偏っている」 との認識を示した。加えて、 「米国
の経済政策の行方や地政学リスクの長期化が見通しへの不確実性を高めている」ほか、 「インフレ
への圧力となり得る」との認識を示した。 
そのうえで、今回の政策決定について「為替レートの動向、同国と米国が異なる景気循環局面に
あること、需要面での物価上昇圧力がないこと、金融引き締め度合いを考慮した」 として、 「現行水
準に維持することが適切」とした。先行きについては、過去３回示してきた『現行水準に維持する
ことが適切』 との文言を削除したうえで、 「ディスインフレ過程や、 価格転嫁、需給の緩み、インフ
レ期待などを踏まえて意思決定を行う」とした。さらに「同国のマクロ経済環境は米国と異なるた
め、 ＦＲＢ （米連邦準備制度理事会） の動きに機械的に反応する必要はない」 としつつ、「低く安定
したインフレ環境を定着させるための取り組みを継続する必要性を強調する」 との考えをあらため
て示した。これは、ＦＲＢが９月のFOMC（連邦公開市場委員会） で利上げを決定したにもかかわら
ず、中銀は米国の政策動向に機械的に追随せず、国内の経済・物価情勢を踏まえて独自に政策判断
を行う考えを示したものと考えられる。

【メキシコ中銀が政策判断の自由度確保を強調したことで､ペソ相場はどうなる？】 
金融市場においては、日本と米国による協調介入の影響により、ペソの対円相場は下振れする動
きが確認されている（図３）。さらに、 Ｆ
ＲＢによる利上げ実施に加え、 先行きも
追加利上げが意識されるなかで米ドル
指数は上昇しており、ペソ安を後押しし
ている。 中銀が今回、 ＦＲＢの動きに追
随しない考えを示したことは、 当面のペ
ソ相場の重しとなる可能性がある。 その
うえ、中銀が警戒する米国の経済政策、
なかでも通商政策に翻弄される可能性
は高く、USMCA（米国・ メキシコ・ カナダ
協定） を巡る動きがペソ相場を左右する
リスクにも注意が必要である。
